In our country, on December 3rd, various events are organised so that we will not forget that we live with them, that we recognize the disabled who find themselves in social life very difficult to deal with and difficult to overcome. On December 3rd, when the isolated events of the disabled who are on the agenda are over, our disabled citizens are being ignored by us as if they never existed in life, and we are moving on with our standard lives. In the cities where we study, we have seen families cope with many problems, such as education and economics. Therefore, it seems necessary for us to have positive discrimination, not only to our disabled citizens, but also to the parents of a disabled person. Because one tenth of our country's population is made up of disabled citizens, and with their families, that number reaches about 25 million. At that time, we must make arrangements for our disabled citizens to have an equal right to education, transportation, employment, as well as to have a say and decision in city politics. The executive is also obliged to do its part. Because there are still setbacks. For example, there are 1,250 disabled teachers waiting for an appointment at the Ministry of National Education. According to the current law, over 35,000 disabled teachers are required to work, while 6,956 disabled teachers are employed in Ministry staffs. What reason has caused 1,250 teachers to wait for months? Is that our empathy? That's our awareness? Is that our positive discrimination? Is that our vision? We expect the Minister to do whatever he has to do at the point where this problem is resolved.

With the number 3788 Tea Law issued in 1940, our country was guaranteed teamaking, and it was required to obtain a license for those who would build a tea garden. After this legal development, tea farms have expanded and production has increased rapidly. Mr. President, the first tea factory was opened in 1947 under the name Rize Central Tea Factory, with a capacity of 60 tons a day. The increase in the production of tea agricultural areas and age tea leaves has also made it necessary to increase the number of tea processing factories, and the number of tea processing factories established in 1973 reached 32. The demand for domestic consumption, which was met with imports until 1963, began to be met with domestic production after 1963. The Law on Tea Association No. 1497 was adopted in 1971. All activities, including agriculture, production and marketing, are referred to the Tea Association General Directorate. The institution began its activities in Rize, 1973, as a state-owned institution with a corporate personality, limited to autonomy and responsibility capital in its activities. The Tea Association was then included as a public economic institution to continue its activities under the name of Law 2929, which was released in 1982. The audit of the OSCE has entered the Constitution of 1961, envisioned that the control of the CSI be regulated by the Turkish Parliament by a special law. In the Constitution of 1982, this sentence was preserved. The OSCE is currently under the Constitution of the Constitution of 1982, under the Constitution of Turkey's Grand National Assembly of Turkey under the Law on the Control of the Funds by Public Economics Act 3346, issued in 1987. The 35-member Commission for Public Economic Initiatives has been established to carry out audits of state-owned state-owned enterprises and partnerships, more than half of its capital with the law. The relevant audit activity is carried out through annual audit reports for each of the institutions controlled by the Court of Justice under the regulations prescribed by Laws 3346 and 6085. The annual audit report prepared by the Court's Presidency with a hundred and fifty-seven years' worth of information and savings is a report that covers all the information, problems, and ways to address the problems of the institutions in comparison with all aspects of the audited institutions, including their views on the balance sheet and results accounts. Each report presents analysis, findings, and suggestions regarding the audited institutions. As I'm about to mention, the OSCE Commission has discussed these proposals one by one and made decisions during the meeting of both the lower commission and the upper commission. Mr. President, the CSI Commission is overseeing the situation of institutions to be audited in accordance with the rules of the economy and the principles of efficiency and profitability in order for them to be useful to the national economy, in order to ensure that they achieve its objectives, in accordance with legislation, in accordance with the long-term development plan and in accordance with the implementation programmes of the plan. The audit is first carried out at the centre of the institutions, with subcommittees set up by the Commission according to its expertise. The report is submitted to the Commission as a result of subcommittee meetings where the institutions, enterprises, factories and facilities are studied in place. The OSCE Commission re-checks and concludes the state of the institutions, combining answers and opinions on the reports of the subcommittee and relevant organisations. In the audits carried out on the file, the administrative boards, executives, Court auditors, representatives of their relevant and connected ministry, the presidency's strategy and budget presidency and the Treasury and Finance Ministry officials and other public institutions are also present and are heard, if necessary. The meeting is first presented by the head of the board of directors and the director general. This presentation provides information on the institution's activities, goals, projects and investments. Later, the findings and proposals included in the Court audit report are being discussed one by one, as necessary proposals are being removed from the agenda, as part of the implementation of proposals are left to follow the Court's presidency, while the proposals that were not carried out are left on the agenda for the following year. The Commission is able to make recommendations by the organisation and the ministry, which ensures that some problems should be resolved or speed up the process, if necessary during the discussion of these proposals, and can ask that an issue be examined by the Ministry's control units, if necessary. During talks on the general issue following the discussion of proposals, the members of the Commission and the MPs who wish are asking questions about the organisation without a deadline. These questions are answered by the company's managers, and the issues that require detailed work are answered in writing within 15 days. Following the completion of the talks, the decision is made on the grounds that the organisation's balance sheet and outcome accounts should be registered or not. A total of 60 meetings per year, 35 subs and 25 tops, are held for inspection of all institutions involved in the audit programme of the year. Following the completion of the control of all institutions, the CSI Commission report is being distributed to all MPs, sent to the presidency, and published on the Commission's page "web" for transparency, and the audit results are announced to the public. And to mention the areas of the operation, the proper age for the business is to buy, produce dry tea, produce and produce dry tea leaves, work technologically to process and evaluate the tea leaves that have been bought, or import the dry teas that it produces, blend them in line with its internal and external desires, packaged and marketed them. TEEYKUR General Directorate has $2 billion and $945 million in capital. The age price of tea is set by the board in accordance with the decision of the general investment and financing programme. The price of the purchase of age teas, according to the basis set in payment, is transferred to our producers' accounts within the next month, and this practice is carried out in all exiles in the same way. The producers' income losses are paid as a reduction compensation due to high-end tea leaves, which are being cut for the purpose of supplying tea leaves. Starting in 2003, the Ministry of Agriculture and Forestry pays tea producers to support the product of age tea leaves by kilograms. It is supported to pay 13 cents per kilogram. Since 2007, organic tea farming has begun, and the amount of organic tea bought for years has increased, as organic areas have increased. It's still produced organic tea with 12,000 producers, 38,000 per cent.
